市場疑慮拖累美股，短線聚焦 Fed 決策與財報
2026 年 9 月 15 日
一、 9/14 美股下挫、美債殖利率曲線熊市趨平，且油價大漲
1. 股票指數：
⚫ 道瓊工業指數下跌 0.29%，收 52,421.20 點；
⚫ S&P 500 指數下挫 0.48%，收 7,619.98 點；
⚫ 那斯達克綜合指數下跌 0.56%，收 26,186.41 點；
⚫ 費城半導體指數下跌 5.86%，收 11,131.28 點。
2. 當日重要個股表現：
⚫ 在科技類股方面，輝達下跌約 3.4%、美光下跌 5.3%、邁威爾下跌 7.3%；

⚫ 通訊類股方面，Netflix 上漲 3.8%、Take-Two 上漲 3.5%、Fox 上漲 4.6%；
⚫ 軟體類股方面 ，ServiceNow、Adobe、Workday 等軟體股則上漲約 4% 至
7.4%。
3. 美國債市漲跌不一、殖利率曲線走勢熊市趨平。2 年期美國公債殖利率上升 3.24 個基
點至 4.66%，10 年期殖利率上升 2.06 個基點至 4.99%，10 年期殖利率一度升破 5%，
而 30 年期殖利率則下跌約 0.76 個基點至 5.34%。短率上升，反映市場對未來美國聯
準會升息的預期。
4. 油價因中東衝突與供給風險再度升高而上漲 ，WTI 原油上漲 1.81%，收在每桶 101.86
美元，布蘭特原油則上漲 1.56%，收在 106.24 美元。
二、 下跌原因分析
1. AI 開發放緩疑慮，壓抑硬體需求預期：Anthropic 執行長 Dario Amodei 於上週末
呼籲放慢前沿 AI 能力提升速度，並提出第三方安全評估與業界協調方案，使 AI 擴

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
張節奏成為新的不確定因素。我們研判，半導體位於 AI 建置支出的上游，股價對新
增算力投資的成長速度尤其敏感。即使既有訂單未立即改變，只要市場降低對未來數
季增速的期待，GPU、高頻寬記憶體及網通設備供應鏈的評價便可能先行調整；目前
仍須區分投資預期降溫與實際訂單下修。
2. 中東能源設施遇襲，油價推升通膨風險：沙烏地阿拉伯能源設施及中東船舶遇襲，引
發供應疑慮，布蘭特原油期貨 9 月 14 日結算價升至每桶 106.24 美元。油價持續偏
高可能透過運輸與生產成本以及民眾實質購買力影響企業獲利；若進一步傳導至通膨

預期，將延長貨幣政策維持緊縮的時間，對股市評價形成壓力。
3. 十年美債殖利率一度突破 5%，評價與融資承壓：美國 10 年期公債殖利率盤中突破
5%，為 2023 年以來首見。殖利率上升提高未來獲利的折現率，對依賴長期成長預
期支撐評價的股票較不利。另一方面，資料中心若仰賴借款擴張，融資成本上升也會
提高投資報酬門檻，可能影響新增建置計畫，形成 AI 供應鏈面臨的另一項壓力。
4. 通膨月增回升 ， 升息前觀望氣氛加重 ：BLS 於 9 月 11 日公布 ，8 月 CPI 月增 0.4%
，高於 7 月的 0.1%，核心 CPI 月增率也由 0.2% 升至 0.3%。我們認為，短期物
價動能回升，加上能源價格偏高，提高聯準會維持緊縮政策的壓力。投資人除了評估
當次升息，也須重新衡量後續利率高點與維持時間，增加 FOMC 會議前高波動股票
的調整壓力。
5. 成長與評價雙重重估，放大半導體跌幅：本波股市回檔壓力同時來自獲利成長預期與
市場願給的評價倍數。AI 開發放緩疑慮影響未來營收想像，殖利率上行則壓低未來獲

利的現值。當股價已反映較高成長期待，只要新增訊息不如預期，即使當期營收仍強
勁，也可能出現明顯修正。後續應觀察獲利上修能否抵銷評價收縮，不能僅以跌幅大
就認定股價便宜。

不另行通知。
三、市場看法
1. 龍頭財報仍具成長，支持中期正向看法：輝達 8 月 26 日公布的 2027 會計年度第
二季資料中心營收達 890 億美元、年增 117%，並預估第三季總營收 1,080 億美元
、上下浮動 2%。博通 9 月 2 日公布的 2026 會計年度第三季 AI 半導體營收為
167 億美元、年增 221%，並預估第四季達 217 億美元。上述實績與公司展望，是
我們對 AI 核心供應鏈維持中期正向看法的依據；惟這些財測發布於本次事件之前，
仍須追蹤後續更新。
2. 放緩方案尚待落地，需求影響須分項評估：Anthropic 執行長 Amodei 明確表示，放

緩不代表停止模型訓練，但也提出研議訓練算力等限制，因此實際影響取決於後續執
行方式。既有模型的商業使用、推論與安全測試仍可能帶來算力需求，惟能否抵銷前
沿訓練投資的放緩，必須由使用量、訂單與收入驗證。此時適合保留成長觀點，同時
提高對支出變化的敏感度。
3. 科技股仍有輪動，軟體與半導體強弱分明：9/14 當日企業軟體與資安股逆勢上漲，顯
示科技股內部仍有輪動與受惠程度的差異。我們認為，若前沿 AI 開發放緩，部分既
有軟體業者面臨的競爭及產品替代壓力可能減輕；AI 安全疑慮升高，也可能強化資安
、權限控管及企業治理需求。相較之下，半導體更直接承受新增建置支出預期變化。
因此，9/14 當日費半重挫尚不足以推論整體科技需求同步轉弱；後續宜比較訂單能見
度、訂閱收入、獲利與現金流，單日相對強勢仍須由基本面延續性驗證。
4. 升息機率約九成，會後政策態度才是關鍵：依 Reuters 於美東 9 月 14 日收盤報導
引述的 CME FedWatch 資料，本次升息一碼的機率約 90%。若美東 9 月 16 日

FOMC 確實升息 0.25 個百分點，利率區間將升至 3.75% 至 4.00%，當次升息已
大致反映於市場預期。我們認為，會後聲明及主席記者會對未來利率政策的態度才是
關鍵：是否暗示還需升息、利率須維持高檔多久，以及是否擔心能源漲價向核心通膨

### Page 4

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
擴散，並須搭配點陣圖判讀。若後續緊縮訊號未超出預期、長債殖利率趨穩，有利評
價修復；若利率路徑較預期更高、更持久，科技股仍可能承壓。
5. 通膨改善仍不均衡，避免過早判定轉折：8 月核心 CPI 年增率為 2.4%，低於 7 月
的 2.5%，但其月增率由 0.2% 升至 0.3%，顯示年增放緩與短期動能回升並存。後
續須觀察能源漲價是否向服務價格與通膨預期擴散。若油價回穩、核心物價再度降溫
，利率壓力可望緩和；若能源衝擊持續，股市調整時間可能拉長。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦 Fed 決策與 10 月美股財報，科技股中期向上趨勢料不致有變：我們目前仍

維持 AI 核心供應鏈中期正向看法，較看好能將營收成長轉化為獲利與現金流的企業
，但宜密切觀察本週的 FOMC 會議結果與 10 月中將起跑的美股財報。若雲端業者維
持資本支出、晶片公司財測穩定，且 FOMC 此次會議未釋出超預期緊縮訊號，有利
市場信心修復；然而，若出現廣泛支出下修、訂單遞延，或油價與殖利率持續上行，
則應拉長觀察期並重新檢視通膨與利率對於股市動能前景的影響。
2. 3~10 年美國公債/投資級債為債市首選：目前美國的通膨數據仍具韌性，且就業市場
穩健，使年底前市場升息預期再度升溫。市場對美國財政赤字、公債供給及長期通膨
仍要求較高風險補償，30 年期殖利率與短天期利差仍維持高檔，顯示期限溢酬尚未收
斂，考量當前 3～10 年期美國公債與投資級債能鎖定目前較高息收，且其受長端財政
風險影響也相對較小，故現階段3～10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對
較佳。

ab 5 October 2026
Global Research
Global I/O
Smartphones
August ’26 Sell-Through: No signs of recovery
yet
Global smartphone unit sell-through -8.9% YoY August '26 YTD
Global smartphone unit sell-through (source: Counterpoint) for the month of August
'26 came in at 85.9m units (-0.1% MoM, -12.3% YoY), marking the ninth consecutive
month of YoY sell-through decline (was -12.2%/-10.9% YoY in July/June). By region, all
regions except for the US (-3.4% YoY) saw double-digit YoY declines, with India seeing

the most incremental downside at -17.7% YoY, followed by China (-17.3%), Europe (-
10.7%) and RoW (-10.1%). Following eight months of significant YoY weakness,
August '26 YTD global smartphone unit sell-through now sits at -8.9% YoY with all
regions well in negative comps (India/China/Europe/RoW/US: -12.3%/-10.5%/-8.7%/-
8.0%/-4.4% YoY).
Apple outperformance continues, Samsung beginning to rebound
Apple: YTD iPhone unit sell-through (149.3m units, +8.7% YoY) continues to highlight

the material outperformance of Apple, which remains the only major OEM (>10% unit
market share) in positive comps. With key iPhone suppliers optimistic on unit growth in
'27 (link) on the back of (1) the change in launch schedule (3 models each in 1Q27 and
3Q27) and (2) the introduction of iPhone Duo (~10m units in first 12 months), we
continue to forecast Apple strength to continue beyond '26 (UBSe sell-in: +8.5% YoY)
and into '27 (+4.7% YoY). Samsung: August '26 sell-through came in at 20.3m units

(+0.9% YoY), returning to positive comps for the first time since March '26, primarily on
the back of the release of the new flagship foldable models (Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, Fold8,
Flip8) released on August 7th. New foldable sales in August came in at 2.8m units, only
0.3m units (or 7.5%) below July+August '25 sales for last year's lineup (Galaxy Z Fold7,
Flip7, Flip7 FE; released on July 25 th, 2025) despite two less weeks of sales. Whilst
YTD sell-through still remains soft at -4.1% YoY, we believe resilient sales of the new

flagship model as well as incremental share gains in the mid-range should help Samsung
achieve YoY unit growth (UBSe '26E sell-in: +4.4% YoY). China Android: We continue
to see memory-driven downside materialising with Transsion, Oppo, Vivo, and Xiaomi
YoY sell-through decline now at -23.1%/-19.0%/-18.2%/-18.1%.
Reiterating our sell-in forecasts of -10.0%/-3.0% YoY in '26E/'27E
We entered the year with a cautious view on the smartphone industry (link) primarily on

the back of rapidly rising memory costs. We also flagged that these dynamics will be
much more penalising for Chinese OEMs focusing on the low-end/mid-range segment.
With the DRAM/NAND upcycles likely to extend until at least 2Q28E/4Q27E and unit
sell-through now down 8.9% YoY globally (and on average 16.0% for Transsion, Oppo,
Vivo, and Xiaomi) eight months into the year, we expect to see double-digit YoY unit
decline % and reiterate our '26E sell-in forecast of -10.0% YoY. Heading into '27, with

memory likely to remain an incremental headwind, we expect to see muted demand for
smartphones and see units declining further from a low base. We recently lowered our
YoY unit sell-in forecasts for '27E to -3.0% from +2.0% (link).
Selective on smartphone plays; prefer memory/MLCC suppliers
Within the smartphone supply chain, we continue to prefer MediaTek and Samsung
(both APAC Key Call Buy) as well as ASE, Hon Hai, JCET, Micron, Murata, SEMCO, SK

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
The handset industry, as part of consumer electronics, is cyclically sensitive to the economy
and so upside and downside risks relate to macro drivers. Other downside risks include the
rapid loss of market share due to poor product timing, design or distribution. The supply
chain companies into the handset industry also operate in a highly cyclical market both from
macro concerns and inventory cycles within the supply chain. These companies also operate

in a highly competitive environment with product innovation and R&D investments critical to
success. Valuation methods across the sector include multiple analysis.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Document 104: 9月贸易数据点评：进出口增速上行，AI、价格为主要拉动

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月贸易数据点评：进出口增速上行，AI、价格为主要拉动.pdf
Report source: 万联证券
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

[Table_RightTitle] 
宏观研究|宏观跟踪报告

[Table_Title] 
进出口增速上行，AI、价格为主要拉动

[Table_ReportType] 
——9月贸易数据点评

[Table_ReportDate] 2026年 10月 14日

[T able_Summary] ⚫ 事件：9月，按美元计价，我国出口当月同比36.3%，前值
25%；进口同比31%，前值28.2%；贸易顺差1335亿美元。 
⚫ 出口增速攀升，外需支撑仍强，产业拉动特征持续。海外制造
业景气度依然较高，韩国出口增速持续走高，半导体出口保持
旺盛。美国8 月制造业库存同比增速继续上行，新订单高位小
幅放缓，经济韧性依然较强，当前加息和通胀的压力尚未显
现。关税预期和全球航运成本持续上升影响下， “抢出口”效
应带来一定推动。高新技术产品、机电产品出口保持上行，AI
产业拉动仍强。我国出口集装箱运价持续走高，出口价格指数
继续上行。人民币持续升值，短期对出口影响较小。 
⚫ 抢出口、基数效应拉动下，对美国出口持续修复。我国对东
盟、非洲、俄罗斯出口保持旺盛。对美出口增速上行，关税政
策不确定性担忧，叠加海外四季度圣诞节等假日备货旺季需求
带动， “抢出口”行为明显。9月中美双方“300亿对300亿”
对等降税政策，给我国轻工消费品出口带来一定利好。欧盟对
我国贸易壁垒政策趋严，未来仍面临一定压力。我国对韩国当
月出口增速大幅上行，主要是AI产业驱动影响。 
⚫ AI 产品出口保持旺盛，汽车出口上行，劳动密集型产品出口
持续改善。集成电路、自动数据处理设备出口金额同比持续攀
升，价格上涨的驱动作用仍强。下游汽车增速上行，新能源汽
车出口退税率2027年 4月即将下调， “抢出口”带来一定推
动；船舶制造业出口持续放缓。劳动密集型产品出口跌幅持续
收窄，低基数和关税政策带来一定拉动。原料端钢材、铝材、
成品油出口上行，稀土持续回落。农产品出口表现分化。 
⚫ 进口上行超市场预期，价格拉动作用加强。进口价格总指数再
度走高，大宗商品、半导体、存储设备等价格上涨对进口的支
持作用加强。AI产业链进口保持旺盛，价格影响下，能源、
金属材料进口表现分化，农产品进口增速回落。 
⚫ 价格、AI 产业拉动效果明显，出口全年增速有望维持高位。
我国全年出口为经济的重要拉动分项，前三季度整体延续上行
趋势。海外需求整体表现较好，劳动密集型产品出口温和修
复，AI产业链呈现较强的拉动作用，但仍需关注半导体、数
据处理设备等产品的价格和库存水平。地缘政治影响下，大宗
商品价格持续高位，价格对进出口的支撑作用仍强。11月将
迎来美国中期选举，未来关税政策仍有调整风险。随着“抢出
口”效应逐步释放，年底出口仍面临一定不确定性，但外需的
支撑作用仍强。 
⚫ 风险因素：海外需求回落幅度超预期，中美贸易关系变动超预
期，地缘政治风险发展超预期。     
[Table_Authors] 分析师: 于天旭 
执业证书编号： S0270522110001 
电话： 17717422697 
邮箱： yutx@wlzq.com.cn

稳增长 
非农就业不及预期，连续加息紧迫性下降 
生产、价格上行共振拉升 PMI

事件 
2026年9月， 按美元计价， 我国出口4471.84亿美元， 当月同比36.3%（市场预期25.7%），
前值25%， 累计同比由19.3%继续上行至21.3%；进口金额3136.52亿美元， 当月同比31%
（市场预期23.8%），前值28.2%，累计同比由27%上行至27.5%；贸易顺差1335.32亿美
元，前值1190.85亿美元，贸易差额当月同比由17.9%上行至50.5%。按人民币计价，
9月出口当月同比29.6%，上月为18.6%，进口同比24.6%，上月为21.7%。

1    出口增速攀升，AI产业链拉动作用延续 
出口增速持续走高，外需支撑仍强，产业拉动特征持续。按美元计价，9月我国出口
4471.84亿美元，当月同比由8月的25%上行至36.3%，显著高于市场预期的25.7%，增
速持续攀升，累计同比由19.3%继续上行至21.3%。美国、日本、欧元区制造业PMI高
位小幅回落，全球制造业PMI上行，海外制造业景气度依然较高。韩国出口增速由68.7%
继续上行至83.5%，半导体出口保持旺盛。美国8月制造业库存同比增速继续上行，新
订单高位小幅放缓，美国经济韧性依然较强，当前加息和通胀的压力尚未显现。关税
预期和全球航运成本持续上升影响下， “抢出口”效应带来一定推动。高新技术产品
出口累计同比由42.9%进一步走高至45.6%，AI产业链出口驱动作用仍强。 机电产品出
口累计同比由26.9%上行至29%，仍是出口主要推动项。

图表1：韩国出口增速高位持续上行（%） 图表2：美国制造业存货持续上行，新增订单回落(%)

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

对东盟、非洲出口保持旺盛，抢出口、基数效应拉动下，对美国出口持续修复，但中
长期贸易关系依然承压。前9个月我国对东盟、非洲、俄罗斯出口增速领跑整体，9月
增速延续高位，出口保持旺盛。对美国出口累计同比上行3.6个百分点至9.8%，当月
同比也继续上行，低基数起到一定抬升作用。美国中期选举将至，关税政策存在一定
不确定性，叠加海外四季度圣诞节等假日备货旺季需求带动， “抢出口”行为明显。
9月中美双方“300亿对300亿”对等降税框架职责文件、降税产品清单等达成共识，
美国降税行业主要为玩具、小家电、婴儿用品、厨卫用品、节日礼物等轻工消费品，
给我国相关行业出口带来一定利好，中国降税则侧重农产品与资源品行业。欧盟对我
国贸易壁垒政策趋严，行业正从新能源领域逐步扩张到化工、风电等传统制造业，虽

然本月出口在企业“抢出口”避险情绪带动下增速上行，但未来仍面临一定压力。我
国对韩国当月出口增速大幅上行，主要是AI产业驱动影响。 
我国出口集装箱运价持续走高，出口价格指数继续上行，进口价格指数反弹。9月我
国出口集装箱运价指数继续攀升至高位，逐步接近2024年7月的高点。红海危机持续
发酵，霍尔木兹海峡航运受阻，船舶航程大幅拉长，叠加燃油成本和附加费增加，推
动运价持续抬升。从已经公布的8月数据来看，我国出口价格总指数上行，矿产品出
口价格反弹上行， 化工相关产品出口价格高位边际回落； 进口价格指数大幅反弹至近
几年高点。

人民币仍处升值通道，短期对出口影响有限。美联储9月开启加息，美元指数持续走
高。但人民币在9月延续升值趋势，美元兑人民币在10月中旬已经向下突破6.7。我国
经济表现稳健， 产业结构持续优化，AI行业快速发展， 出口保持旺盛，贸易顺差1335.32
亿美元，前值1190.85亿美元，环比持续扩张，企业结售汇意愿较强，央行外汇占款
同比稳步上行。我国对外出口市场多元化布局，产业链、价格优势明显，企业外汇套
保策略可规避部分外汇风险。但四季度为传统外贸结汇旺季，人民币升值的背景下，
出口企业仍面临一定汇兑损失的风险。

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 5 页 共 8 页  
AI相关产品出口保持旺盛，汽车出口上行，劳动密集型产品出口持续改善，资源品出
口整体增长，农产品出口表现分化。从出口商品来看，前9个月，我国AI产业链出口
保持旺盛，集成电路出口金额累计同比由103.9%继续攀升至108.1%， 自动数据处理设
备由49.4%上行至54.6%，价格上涨的驱动作用仍强。下游制造业出口分化，但仍具韧
性，船舶出口由35.2%回落至29.9%，跌幅较大；汽车和汽车底盘由53.2%上行至54.3%，
汽车零件也有所上行，新能源汽车出口退税率2027年4月即将下调， “抢出口”带来一
定推动。手机、音视频设备出口增速上行。劳动密集型产品出口跌幅持续收窄，基数
走低和中美“300亿对300亿”对等降税政策带来一定拉动，鞋靴、玩具、灯具等跌幅
收窄，服饰、箱包、家具等增速保持上行。原料端出口大多上行，地缘政治扰动下，
原油价格持续上行，成品油出口增速本月上行幅度相对最大，钢材、铝材增速上行，
稀土在政策影响下高位逐步回落。农产品方面，粮食出口增速小幅回落，水产品出口
上行转正；肥料出口止跌反弹，磷肥涨价形成一定推动，但增速依然偏低，主要受出
口管控政策影响。

图表7：出口分项累计同比增速走势（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

2    进口增速超市场预期，价格拉动作用加强 
进口增速上行超市场预期，价格支撑作用加强。2026年9月，我国进口金额3136.52亿
美元，按美元计价，当月同比由28.2%上行至31%，远高于市场预期的23.8%，累计同
比由27%上行至27.5%。按人民币计价，9月进口同比24.6%，上月为21.7%。进口价格
总指数再度走高，商品价格指数同比持续上行，大宗商品、半导体、存储设备等价格
上涨对进口的支持作用加强。 我国对韩国进口增速由72.5%进一步走高至75.4%，AI产
业发展带动效果仍强。 我国对美国进口增速延续修复， 对东盟国家进口高位继续上行。
对巴西、澳大利亚等资源国进口高位边际放缓，价格上涨带来一定影响。我国对欧盟
进口增速持续承压下滑。整体来看，价格、政策为进口主要影响因素。

AI产业链进口保持旺盛，价格影响下，能源、金属材料进口表现分化，农产品进口增
速回落。从进口产品来看，前9个月，我国机电产品进口累计同比增速由31.6%上行至
33.3%，高技术产品进口累计同比由40.1%上行至42.2%，与出口同步走强。自动数据
处理设备增速上行18.5个百分点至115%， 上行幅度相对最大， 集成电路进口增速也有
所上行。汽车零件、汽车和汽车底盘进口跌幅进一步加深，飞机进口增速低位下探。
农产品进口整体回落，其中食用植物油进口反弹上行，大豆、鲜、干水果及坚果增速
回落。能源类进口分化，受油价高位波动影响，成品油增速进一步回落，而原油价格
小幅反弹，煤及褐煤增速保持上行，天然气进口持续修复。上游资源品进口也表现分
化，铜矿砂、铁矿砂进口增速回落，钢材、铜材、原木、纸浆进口增速上行。

3    总结与展望 
价格、AI产业拉动效果明显，出口全年增速有望维持高位。我国全年出口为经济的重
要拉动分项，前三季度整体延续上行趋势。海外需求整体表现较好，劳动密集型产品
出口温和修复，AI产业链呈现较强的拉动作用，但仍需关注半导体、数据处理设备等

海外需求回落幅度超预期，中美贸易关系变动超预期，地缘政治风险发展超预期。

Document 156: AI资本开支暗藏“租赁账单”！摩根士丹利将2027年投资额上调至1.5万亿美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI资本开支暗藏“租赁账单”！摩根士丹利将2027年投资额上调至1.5万亿美元.pdf

### Page 1

⼤摩表⽰，若将未体现在现⾦流中的租赁资本开⽀纳⼊统计， 2027 年全球 AI 计算投资总规
模将升⾄ 1.58 万亿美元，较现⾦⼝径扩⼤ 14% 。⾼额投资将⼤幅驱动标普 500 营收与盈
利，其中英伟达独占约三分之⼆流⼊份额。不过，融资压⼒正加剧⾼低评级主体分化，低
评级云服务商⾯临更⼤的融资约束与成本敏感性。
摘要：
当传统资本开⽀⼝径不再完整，租赁融资正悄然重塑 AI 基础设施投资的全貌。
据追⻛交易台消息，据摩根⼠丹利 Nicholas Lentini 团队的最新研究报告，将租赁资本开⽀纳⼊
统计后，超⼤规模云服务商（ hyperscaler ）及新兴云服务商（ neocloud ） 2027 年 AI 计算投资
总规模升⾄约 1.58 万亿美元，较现⾦资本开⽀⼝径扩⼤约 14% 。这⼀数字意味着 2026 年⾄ 2027
年间， AI 投资增量⾼达约 7000 亿美元，增速约 60% 。
摩根⼠丹利指出，这 1.58 万亿美元的资本开⽀将直接带动标普 500 指数约 5% 的 2027 年营收，并

贡献约 25% 的营收增速。更⼴泛看，被摩根⼠丹利归类为 AI 核⼼受益者的 " 使能者 " （ Enablers ）
与 " 采⽤者 " （ Adopters ）合计将驱动标普 500 指数 78% 的 2027 年盈利增⻓。值得注意的是，英伟
达⼀家公司预计将独占标普 500 指数 AI 资本开⽀流⼊规模的约三分之⼆。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

研究显⽰，超⼤规模云服务商已披露逾 1.1 万亿美元尚未启动的租赁承诺。由于数据中⼼租赁属
于债务融资性质的资产购置，在现⾦流量表中不体现为资本开⽀，亦不进⼊管理层指引，导致
市场⻓期低估 AI 实际投资规模。将租赁项⽬纳⼊后，摩根⼠丹利估计 2026 年、 2027 年、 2028 年
AI 总资本开⽀分别约为 9800 亿美元、 1.58 万亿美元及 1.81 万亿美元，同⽐增速分别约为 100% 、
60% 及 14% 。
租赁资本开⽀： " 影⼦账单 " 有多⼤
摩根⼠丹利在本次报告中⾸次系统引⼊超⼤规模云服务商的租赁资本开⽀预测。根据其估算，
2027 年租赁资本开⽀达 1950 亿美元， 2028 年进⼀步扩⼤⾄ 2460 亿美元。从单家公司看， 2027
年甲⻣⽂贡献最⼤，达 572 亿美元，其次依次为微软（ 453 亿美元）、 META （ 372 亿美元）、亚⻢
逊（ 284 亿美元）及⾕歌（ 266 亿美元）。
这⼀问题的核⼼在于会计处理⽅式。超⼤规模云服务商通过租赁⽅式获取数据中⼼的⾏为，在

经济实质上等同于债务融资购置资产，但并不体现在现⾦流量表的资本开⽀科⽬下，也不纳⼊
管理层的资本开⽀指引。摩根⼠丹利认为，这导致传统资本开⽀预测 " 越来越难以还原资本投⼊
的全貌 " 。
从投资回报⻆度看，摩根⼠丹利对这批投资持乐观态度。报告估计，新增计算容量的投资回报
率（ ROIC ）在 25% ⾄ 50% 之间，并测算了 GPU 租赁（ IaaS ）及模型 API 两类业务模式 —— 前者
可实现 60% ⾄ 70% 的边际 EBIT 利润率及 25% ⾄ 40% 的 ROIC ，后者增量 EBIT 利润率可超 70% ，
ROIC 最⾼可达 63% 。

### Page 3

AI 资本开⽀流向：谁在分⻝这块蛋糕
摩根⼠丹利通过⾃下⽽上的⽅式，将 1.58 万亿美元资本开⽀追踪⾄约 50 家公司的营收，覆盖科
技、通信服务、⼯业、能源及海外 ODM 等板块。按其测算，约 65% 的 AI 资本开⽀最终流⼊标普
500 成分股营收，其中英伟达占据流⼊规模的约三分之⼆。
在各受益板块中， AI 芯⽚、内存、⽹络及 ODM （原始设计制造商）获得最⼤份额，英伟达、三
星、 SK hynix 及美光合计拿⾛其中绝⼤部分。
芯⽚层⾯，摩根⼠丹利认为英伟达具备最佳⻛险回报⽐，理由是公司正处于新⼀代产品周期爬
坡早期， Fenyman 平台有望推动每 GW 算⼒对应营收超过 500 亿美元，且当前估值约 15 倍 FY28
EPS ，具备估值扩张空间。博通亦受益于定制硅业务扩张，预计 2028 年 XPU 市场规模将突破 1 万
亿美元。
内存层⾯，DRAM 已成为 AI 建设的关键瓶颈，但相关股票仅以约 3 倍 NTM 市盈率交易，隐含增⻓
溢价⼏乎为零。摩根⼠丹利判断内存周期将于 2026 年四季度进⼊后期，价格涨幅趋于温和，供

给增加将限制盈利惊喜空间。 SK hynix 与 Samsung 已通过⻓期协议（ LTA ）锁定⼤部分需求，

### Page 4

SK hynix 承诺回购并注销 400 亿韩元股份， Samsung 则设定 2026 年 900 亿⾄ 1100 亿韩元的股东
回报⽬标。
⽹络层⾯，每增加 1 美元超⼤规模云服务商资本开⽀，约对应 0.25 美元的⽹络营收，预计 2027 年
⽹络总营收约 3740 亿美元。在覆盖的主要公司中，英伟达（⽹络业务）、博通、 Innolight 及
Arista Networks 预计位居超⼤规模云相关⽹络营收前列。
设计软件层⾯，超⼤规模云服务商持续加⼤定制硅投⼊，为 Synopsys 和 Cadence 提供增量机
会，尤其是 Synopsys 新推出的应⽤优化 IP （ AOIP ）模式，通过⽣产版税将营收与芯⽚出货量更
直接挂钩。
数据中⼼建设引发的电⼒需求正成为⼯业及电⼒设备商的重要增⻓引擎。摩根⼠丹利指出， AI 资
本开⽀对⼯业板块的传导路径集中于电⼒供应与冷却设备。
电⼒设备⽅⾯，GE Vernova 燃⽓轮机已售罄⾄ 2029 年，售价从 2020 年代初的每千⽡ 1000 美元
涨⾄⽬前的每千⽡ 3000 美元，数据中⼼相关订单已占其管道订单的约三分之⼀。 INIO 与 BE 则分
别受益于⾼速往复式发动机及燃料电池需求。摩根⼠丹利认为，上调资本开⽀将进⼀步延伸电
⼒ OEM 的积压订单并提升利润率。
融资压⼒：⾼评级与低评级主体分化加剧
随着资本开⽀持续扩张，⾃由现⾦流缺⼝正在拉⼤。摩根⼠丹利估计，2027 年将是预测期内现
⾦流⾚字最⼤的⼀年。超⼤规模云服务商今年以来已发⾏约 2550 亿美元⽆担保债券，约占全年
预估资本开⽀的 30% ，但 10 年期债券全价收益率较 2025 年底上升约 100 ⾄ 180 个基点。
摩根⼠丹利将融资主体分为两个层次：⾼评级超⼤规模云服务商（亚⻢逊、 META 、⾕歌、微
软）⽬前利息覆盖倍数约 30.7 倍，即便 2027 年新增发债 4000 亿美元，覆盖倍数仍可维持在 11.9
倍，对融资成本基本不敏感；⽽低评级主体（甲⻣⽂、 SPCX ）的覆盖倍数仅约 4 倍，若同期新
增发债 1200 亿美元，覆盖倍数将降⾄约 1.8 倍，⾯临较⼤融资约束。
新兴云服务商（ neocloud ）主要依赖项⽬层⾯融资及可转债，是对融资成本最敏感的群体，通
常需要借助⾼评级交易对⼿的信⽤⽀持来获取融资。
从融资结构演变看，摩根⼠丹利预计 2027 年发债期限将向短端倾斜，芯⽚和服务器因现⾦流产
⽣速度较快，更适合以资产⽀持融资及私募形式匹配，具备与合同需求挂钩的灵活性。

Document 175: Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence #aud #australia
Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
14:15 October 13 2026 Share
Confidence posts its sharpest fall since the start of the Middle East conflict as elevated fuel
prices and higher interest rates weigh on business sentiment, while conditions hold steady
at weak levels. Conditions unchanged at -1 / Confidence -4pts to -13.

### Page 2

The September NAB Business Survey was in the field from 23 to 30 September, capturing ongoing
global uncertainty, still-rising fuel costs and, at least in part, the RBA’s September rate hike. Business
confidence continued to deteriorate, recording its sharpest fall since March and the onset of the Middle
East conflict. The index declined 4pts to -13 and, while remaining above the lows recorded earlier this
year, is now at its weakest level since May. At 18pts below its long-run average, the result underscores

the deep pessimism that continues to weigh on businesses. This broadly mirrors the pessimism evident
in the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index, where elevated fuel prices and higher
interest rates have coalesced in a similarly weak assessment of the outlook.
The headline business conditions index held steady in negative territory at -1, albeit rising 0.25pts on
an unrounded basis. While encouraging that conditions have not deteriorated further, the index has

now remained at -1 (rounded) for two consecutive months, its weakest level since the height of the
COVID-19 pandemic in 2020. At 7pts below its long-run average, the result points to continued
pressure across the business sector.
Across the major sub-components, employment moved into negative territory, falling 3pts to -1 and
sitting 4pts below its long-run average. This marks the first outright negative reading since November
2020 and provides further evidence of the gradual easing in labour market conditions. Against the

backdrop of compressed margins, the result may also indicate firms are becoming increasingly
cautious around hiring decisions. By contrast, trading conditions rose 1pt to 4, although they remain
8pts below their long-run average. The relative resilience in trading conditions is broadly consistent with

### Page 3

Westpac DataX Card-Tracker data, which suggests nominal spending remained resilient, albeit
slowing, through the September quarter despite ongoing headwinds facing consumers. Profitability
recorded the strongest improvement across the major components, rising 3pts to -5, although the sub-
index remains deeply subdued at 10pts below its long-run average.
Capacity utilisation edged 0.2ppt lower to 82.3%. While the monthly decline provides a tentative signal

of easing capacity pressures, utilisation remains 0.9ppt above its long-run average and continues to
trend higher on a three-month average basis. Capital expenditure continued to recover from its mid-
year weakness, rising 3pts to 7. While still below the highs recorded in the first quarter, the sub-index is
now trending higher on a three-month average basis, pointing to some stabilisation in investment
intentions.
Cost growth was comparatively subdued in September, although purchase cost pressures remain

elevated. Purchase cost growth edged 0.1ppt higher to 2.4%qtr, sitting 1.1ppt above its long-run
average and continuing to weigh on margins. By contrast, labour cost growth eased 0.1ppt to 1.7%qtr,
although it remains 0.5ppt above its long-run average. Final product price growth was unchanged at
0.8%qtr, 0.2ppt above its long-run average. The widening gap between purchase cost and final product
price growth suggests firms continue to absorb a meaningful share of higher input costs, consistent

with profitability remaining deeply below average. Looking ahead, the October survey will provide an
early indication of how the card surcharging ban is affecting business costs and the extent to which
these are passed through to final prices.

### Page 4

Related articles
Among other indicators, forward orders declined 1pt to -1, pointing to continued caution around the
demand outlook. Inventories, by contrast, rose 4pts (rounded) to 7, some 5pts above their long-run
average. In combination with weak forward orders, the inventory build points to a degree of slack
emerging across the business sector.

Document 212: CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng Không Taseco - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-22)

### Page 1

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco (AST) 
Tiếp tục mở rộng điểm kinh doanh 
22/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD tăng trưởng tích cực trong 
2Q2026

Tăng trưởng khách qua cảng hàng 
không thúc đẩy doanh thu tại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu

Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng 
trưởng doanh thu mới

Cơ cấu kinh doanh và hiệu quả vận 
hành hỗ trợ cải thiện biên lợi nhuận

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá mục 
tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, AST ghi nhận doanh thu và LNST đạt 479/92 tỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), với 
động lực chủ yếu đến từ (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn 
Nhất và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng chờ thương gia. Lũy kế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đạt 1,035/221 tỷ VND 
(+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa tại các thị trường AST khai thác 
tăng bình quân 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. Lưu lượng và tỷ trọng khách quốc tế 
gia tăng được kỳ vọng thúc đẩy số giao dịch và doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi 
khách, đặc biệt tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Chúng tôi dự phóng số điểm kinh doanh AST tăng từ 145 điểm cuối năm 2026 lên 
182 điểm năm 2030. Sau đóng góp từ 19 điểm tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất và 6 điểm mới tại 
T2 Nội Bài từ 2025 - 2026, các dự án Long Thành, Phú Quốc và Gia Bình tiếp tục mở 
ra dư địa mở rộng mạng lưới trong trung và dài hạn cho doanh nghiệp.

Việc mở rộng các hoạt động mang lại lợi nhuận cao, bao gồm hàng miễn thuế, 
phòng chờ thương gia và tối ưu hoạt động tại các điểm kinh doanh được kỳ vọng 
tác động tích cực lên biên lợi nhuận của AST. Chúng tôi dự phóng biên lợi nhuận 
gộp đạt 64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027, hỗ trợ LNST công ty mẹ tăng lần lượt 
48%/17% yoy.

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với AST, giá mục tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 21/09/2026.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco là một 
trong những doanh nghiệp có quy mô mạng 
lưới điểm kinh doanh lớn trong lĩnh vực dịch 
vụ phi hàng không tại Việt Nam, với 141 
điểm kinh doanh tại 6 sân bay quốc tế tính 
đến cuối tháng 6/2026. Hoạt động cốt lõi của 
công ty bao gồm bán lẻ bách hóa lưu niệm 
(BHLN), hàng miễn thuế, dịch vụ ăn uống và 
phòng chờ thương gia. Ngoài ra, AST còn 
tham gia lĩnh vực suất ăn hàng không thông 
qua khoản đầu tư tại công ty liên kết VinaCS. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

HOẠT ĐỘNG KINH DOANH 
KQKD tăng trưởng tích cực trong 
2Q2026

2Q2026, AST ghi nhận doanh thu và LNST đạt 479/92 tỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), 
với động lực: (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất 
và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng chờ thương gia. Lũy kế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đạt 1,035/221 tỷ 
VND (+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Biểu đồ 5. Doanh thu/điểm kinh doanh của AST

Nguồn:AST

Tăng trưởng khách qua cảng hàng 
không thúc đẩy doanh thu tại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng tăng trưởng lưu lượng hành khách, đặc biệt là sự gia tăng tỷ 
trọng khách quốc tế, sẽ thúc đẩy doanh thu và hiệu quả kinh doanh của AST 
trong giai đoạn 2026–2030. Theo Cục Hàng không Việt Nam, lượng khách thông 
qua các cảng hàng không trong 7T2026 đạt hơn 76 triệu lượt (+7% yoy), trong 
đó khách quốc tế đạt gần 30 triệu lượt (+11% yoy), trong khi khách nội địa ghi 
nhận tăng trưởng 4% yoy. Xu hướng này được hỗ trợ bởi nhu cầu du lịch quốc 
tế, chính sách thị thực thuận lợi và việc mở rộng mạng bay, trong khi tăng 
trưởng kinh tế và thu nhập tiếp tục tạo nền tảng cho nhu cầu đi lại trong nước. 
Trên cơ sở đó, chúng tôi dự phóng lượt khách quốc tế và nội địa tại các thị 
trường AST khai thác tăng bình quân lần lượt 11% và 5%/năm đến 2030, qua đó 
nâng dần tỷ trọng khách quốc tế trong tổng lưu lượng, thúc đẩy doanh 
thu/điểm kinh doanh nhờ: (1) lưu lượng hành khách qua cảng hàng không gia 
tăng và (2) tỷ trọng khách quốc tế cao hơn hỗ trợ doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi 
khách, đặc biệt tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

 Biểu đồ 7. Doanh thu/điểm kinh doanh AST (tỷ VND)

Nguồn: AST  Nguồn: AST

Mở rộng mạng lưới tại các nhà ga 
hiện hữu và sân bay mới, gia tăng 
quy mô kinh doanh đến 2030 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng chu kỳ mở rộng hạ tầng hàng không giai đoạn 2026–2030 sẽ 
tạo dư địa gia tăng quy mô kinh doanh cho AST, với số điểm kinh doanh dự 
phóng đạt 145 điểm vào cuối 2026 và tăng lên 182 điểm vào năm 2030. Cụ thể: 
- Trong ngắn hạn, tăng trưởng năm 2026 được hỗ trợ bởi việc ghi nhận 
đủ năm doanh thu từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất 
trong năm 2025, cùng 6 điểm mới tại T2 Nội Bài trong năm 2026, trong 
đó có 2 cửa hàng miễn thuế và 2 cửa hàng Memories of Vietnam khai 
trương trong 2Q2026. 
- Trong trung và dài hạn, các dự án Cảng HKQT Long Thành, Cảng HKQT 
Phú Quốc nâng cấp mở rộng và Cảng HKQT Gia Bình sẽ góp phần thúc 
đẩy ngành hàng không Việt Nam nói chung, bổ sung mặt bằng thương 
mại, mở ra cơ hội phát triển các mảng kinh doanh cốt lõi cho AST nói 
riêng bao gồm các mảng bán lẻ, miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood và 
phòng chờ thương gia. Với kinh nghiệm khai thác 141 điểm kinh doanh 
tại 6 sân bay quốc tế và lịch sử trúng thầu tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất, T2 Nội 
Bài trong thời gian qua, chúng tôi kỳ vọng AST có khả năng cạnh tranh 
cao trong việc đấu thầu quyền khai thác mặt bằng tại các sân bay mới, 
đặc biệt là Gia Bình, qua đó duy trì vị thế và thị phần kinh doanh phi 
hàng không tại khu vực phía Bắc khi một phần lưu lượng hành khách 
được phân bổ lại từ Nội Bài.

Cơ cấu kinh doanh và hiệu quả vận 
hành hỗ trợ mở rộng biên lợi nhuận  
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng biên lợi nhuận của AST tiếp tục cải thiện trong 2026 - 2027, 
nhờ định hướng mở rộng các mảng có biên lợi nhuận tốt như hàng miễn thuế 
và phòng chờ thương gia, kết hợp tối ưu hiệu quả vận hành tại các điểm kinh 
doanh. Cụ thể: (1) AST ưu tiên gia tăng số điểm kinh doanh hàng miễn thuế 
nhằm đón đầu tăng trưởng nhanh và ổn định của lượng khách quốc tế, qua đó 
nâng đóng góp lợi nhuận từ hoạt động mua sắm tại sân bay, (2) mở rộng mạng 
lưới phòng chờ, đồng thời gia tăng lượng khách sử dụng trên nền năng lực phục 
vụ hiện hữu và (3) tối ưu hóa hiệu quả hoạt động thông qua cải thiện bố trí mặt 
bằng, danh mục sản phẩm và kiểm soát chi phí tại các điểm kinh doanh, qua 
đó tăng doanh thu và lợi nhuận thu được trên mỗi khách hàng.

mục tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu AST, với giá mục tiêu 76,500 
VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 
21/09/2026, dựa trên triển vọng tăng trưởng từ lượng khách quốc tế, mở rộng 
mạng lưới điểm kinh doanh và khả năng cải thiện biên lợi nhuận của doanh 
nghiệp. Chúng tôi sử dụng phương pháp định giá từng phần (SOTP), bao gồm 
DCF cho hoạt động kinh doanh cốt lõi của AST và P/E mục tiêu 11x đối với phần 
lợi nhuận thuộc sở hữu AST tại VinaCS. Các giả định chính gồm: (1) lượng khách 
quốc tế/nội địa tăng trung bình 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030, (2) số điểm kinh 
doanh tăng từ 145 điểm cuối 2026 lên 182 điểm năm 2030, với giả định AST 
trúng thầu mặt bằng tại cả Long Thành và Gia Bình và (3) biên lợi nhuận gộp 
2026/2027 đạt 64.7/65.5%. Đối với VinaCS, chúng tôi kỳ vọng nhà máy suất ăn 
Long Thành sẽ hỗ trợ tăng trưởng lợi nhuận trong trung và dài hạn khi dự kiến 
có thể bắt đầu đóng góp lợi nhuận cho AST từ 2028.

Rủi ro đối với định giá bao gồm: (1) AST không giành được quyền khai thác tại 
Long Thành và Gia Bình như kỳ vọng, (2) lưu lượng khách dịch chuyển giữa các 
sân bay mới và hiện hữu khiến doanh thu tại một số điểm kinh doanh bị sụt 
giảm, (3) cạnh tranh gia tăng từ các doanh nghiệp kinh doanh dịch vụ phi hàng 
không khác và (4) căng thẳng địa chính trị kéo dài, giá dầu tăng gây áp lực lên 
hoạt động hàng không và chi tiêu của hành khách.

ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION
Stronger trade posture pays off. The EU and China have agreed to
restrain hybrid cars exports to the EU to a level half or less than cur -
rently projected over the next four years, cutting exports by “several
million units” though still allowing them to rise. China also agreed to

drop its tariffs on EUR 4 bn of EU exports and provide easier access to
Chinese rare earths and magnets under a fast-track licensing sche -
me. These announcements were made at the conclusion of EU Trade
Commissioner Maroš Šefcovic’s visit to Beijing, following a recent es -
calation in trade tensions. Meanwhile, France and Germany released
a joint non-paper on Strengthening European resilience, addressing
global macroeconomic imbalances and market distorting practices

calling notably for:
i) Broadening the tools the European Commission may use to address
unfair trade practices (including undervalued exchange rates) or im -
port surges;
ii) Procedural changes to make them easier to use (qualified majority
of member states needed to block rather than authorise them). These
measures will be discussed during the next EU council on October
15-16th.
The European Commission also unveiled a new critical materials pac -

kage involving 46 strategic extraction, processing or recycling projects
spread across 16 member states (including 5 in France). The goal is to
reduce the EU’s dependence on China for critical metals.
EUROZONE
Price pressures are broadening, activity holds up. Producer price in -
flation accelerated further in August (8.3% y/y 1 from 5.8%), driven by
energy. Pressures are becoming more widespread, notably in capital
goods and above all intermediate goods (6.8%). They are partly offset

by non-durable consumer goods, which remain in deflation (-0.8%).
Given the PPI's close correlation with the HICP , this points to further
upside risks to consumer inflation. On the activity front, final September
PMIs confirmed the flash estimates, with the composite index still solid
at 53.1 and the manufacturing index stable at 52.7. The construction
PMI improved but remains depressed (43.4; +0.4 pt). Retail sales edged
up 0.1% m/m2 in August.

France
Modest spread relief as budget positioning starts. The spread between
French and German 10Y yields narrowed to 136 bps 3 on October 9
(mostly thanks to a decline in the French yield). As the Finance Com -
mittee of the National Assembly started discussing the 2027 budget,
the government reiterated its strong intent not to deviate from the
5% of GDP deficit target and to get a budget adopted in time, likely
without a vote. This is the most likely outcome — but not to be im -

plemented before 10 December (given mandatory timelines) — which
effectively removes any incentive for MPs to back unpopular measures
during the process. We can therefore expect heated debates (including
multiple no-confidence votes being tabled but rejected) and a lack in
fiscal discipline. In parallel, the "Rassemblement National" party ("Na-
tional Rally" in English) presented its fiscal strategy, which includes an
alternative budget for 2027 and an aggressive fiscal consolidation path

throughout 2032. The party called for a referendum to constitutionalise
1 Year-on-year
2 Month-on-month
3 Basis points
a “golden rule” that would require a deficit at least 0.5 pp below the
debt-stabilising threshold (roughly 3% of GDP), as long as the public
debt ratio exceeds 60% of GDP . The golden rule would require a budget
balance adjustment of 0.5 pp per year if the fiscal deficit is more than
1 pp above the stabilising threshold. The adjustment measures put

forward lack specifics but likely overstate savings, understate costs
and rely on overly optimistic growth assumptions (despite large fiscal
consolidation, at least 1.7% annually over the next three years — a
pace reached only 4 times in France since 2008, apart from post-pan-
demic reopening, and well in excess of the roughly 1% growth potential
estimated by most international institutions). That said, it is significant
that this party has chosen to fly the flag for fiscal discipline loud and

clear. Other parties should do the same.
Activity is holding up. New vehicle registrations rose 11% y/y in Sep -
tember, confirming a marked acceleration over the past three months
(+9.4% y/y in Q3). In Q3, growth came entirely from electric vehicles
(+112% y/y), whose share of registrations reached an all-time high
(38% in Q3, 41% in September). Export growth continued in August,
both in services (+5.2% y/y YTD) and in goods (+5.6% y/y YTD) — though

driven by prices rather than volume in the latter case. Manufacturing
output posted only a moderate rebound in August (+0.3% m/m; fol -
lowing cumulative declines of 2.8% over the previous three months),
limited by renewed supply constraints in Q3 (which limited produc -
tion at 31.5% of companies; +5 pp compared to H1). Industrial produc-
tion fell (-0.3% m/m) as a result of a decline in electricity production
(-2.4% m/m) driven by the heatwave. Credit growth remains dynamic.

Bank and market financing to NFCs saw steady growth in August (+4%).
New housing loans rebounded slightly in August (EUR 12.2 bn) fol -
lowing a relatively weak July, whilst interest rates continued to rise.
Consumer credit remained buoyant.
Upcoming: Banque de France monthly Business Survey (Monday), Bu-
siness creations in September (Friday).
Germany
Bullish official growth forecast upgrade despite mixed data. The
German government raised its 2026 GDP growth forecast to 1.3%

— the fastest pace since 2017 — from a prior estimate of 0.5%, citing
stronger-than-expected exports and a surge in government spending.
The 2027 forecast was also raised to 1.1% from 0.9%. Industrial pro -
duction was stronger than expected in August (+2.3% y/y versus -1.7%
in July; the highest level since March 2025 and higher than antici -
pated), driven mainly by strong growth in construction output, prima -
rily in public works (+10.5%; to the highest level since November 2021).

By contrast, manufacturing output fell by -0.2% (after +3.3% in July)
due to a decline in transport equipment production (lowest level since
April 2022). Likewise, new factory orders hit their lowest level in one
year in August (-10.6% m/m). However, this decline is mainly due
to a base effect. Export growth remains solid: +4.4% YTD, despite a
-0.8% m/m decline in August. Exports to the Eurozone continue to rise
(+6.3% y/y YTD), while exports to the U.S. have returned to positive

territory (+1.1%) and those to China continue to decline (-12.9%). The
trade surplus reached EUR 19.5 bn in August (+4% y/y YTD, despite a
-9.8% m/m decline).

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026

ECONEWS Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
UNITED STATES
Mild slowdown in services and more rate hikes signaled. The non-ma-
nufacturing ISM survey fell 0.5 pp4 to 54.9 in September due to a sharp
slowdown in the business activity component (down 5.2 pp to 56.5 pp,
still a robust expansion). Employment rose and supply pressures in -
tensified for the first time since June, while the price gauge reached

its highest level (74, up 1.4 pp) since 2022. Meanwhile, the minutes
of the September FOMC meeting confirmed the Fed’s hawkish outlook
and noted that the AI-buildout carried growing weight in the reading of
current inflation data and outlook. Governor Waller and St. Louis Fed
President Musalem mentioned the need for additional rate hikes while
remaining flexible on timing. The New York Fed survey of consumer in-
flation expectations showed them rising to 3.9%. We continue to expect

two more hikes, the next one coming in December.
Upcoming: September CPI (Wednesday), September PPI and Retail
Sales (Thursday), September Industrial Production (Friday).
JAPAN
Solid wage growth fails to lift household spending. Total wage growth
eased to 3.8% y/y in August (down 0.5 pp) as bonus payments stalled
(0% y/y, after 5.3% y/y in July). This allowed continued real wage growth
at 1.5% y/y, extending an uninterrupted run of positive readings in 2026

as administrative measures kept inflation in check. However, this did
not prevent real household spending from contracting in August for the
ninth consecutive month (-3.1% y/y).
EMERGING MARKETS
The IIF reports that nonresident portfolio flows to EMs turned negative
at -USD 26.3 bn in September, after USD 11.4 bn in August, the first net
outflow since June, as a hawkish Fed lifted US real yields and the USD.
Equity outflows reached -USD 19.2 bn, led by heavy foreign selling of

Korean equities, while debt flows turned negative at -USD 7.0 bn as
hard currency demand faded and EM credit spreads widened late in
the month. Further hikes from DM central banks will create additional
headwinds, but EM should remain resilient as long as global growth
persists.
ASIA
China
Yuan policy defence. Coinciding with the Beijing visit of the EU trade
commissioner, the People's Bank of China issued an unusually lengthy
formal statement rejecting claims that the yuan is undervalued and

defending its exchange-rate management by reiterating that it has a
‘floating exchange rate regime’ with reference to a basket of currencies.
It further argued that China's trade surpluses are driven by rising in -
dustrial competitiveness rather than currency under-devaluation and
that exchange-rate adjustments will not resolve structural global im -
balances. Nonetheless, it allowed the yuan to hit a four-year high vs.
the CFETS basket — bringing its year-to-date gain to 5.7%. The PBOC

set the USD/CNY's reference rate at 6.7330, the strongest level since
2023.
India
Monetary tightening. The RBI raised its repo rate by 25bp to 5.5%, its
first hike since February 2023, and shifted to a “calibrated tightening”
stance amidst rising inflationary pressures. The RBI introduced new
measures to stabilise the rupee, including direct dollar supplies to
state-owned oil companies and tighter restrictions on FX derivatives.
4 Percentage points

EUROPE, MIDDLE EAST, AFRICA
Central Europe
Cautious monetary policy stance amid mild inflation uptick. Poland’s
and Romania’s Central banks kept policy rates unchanged at 3.75% and
6.5%, respectively. Inflation accelerated in September, with a mild up-
tick in the Czech Republic and Hungary (respectively 2.5% and 1.6% y/y)
and a more entrenched increase in Poland (4% y/y). Inflation is now
above the official target in the Czech Rep. and Poland, but fuel price

caps in both countries should contain inflation dynamics.
Gulf
PMIs for September point for a firm but uneven non-oil recovery. Both
Saudi and UAE PMIs stood at 55.3, driven mostly by export orders in
the UAE and by domestic demand in Saudi Arabia. On the contrary,
Qatar’s PMI fell again (to 46.7), while Kuwait’s PMI also declined but
remained in expansionary zone (at 52.4).
Türkiye
Manufacturing production contracted by 1.6% m/m in August, the lar-

gest monthly decrease since the beginning of the year. On a y/y ba -
sis, it fell by 2.3%. Both domestic demand and exports seem to have
contributed negatively to this poor outcome. On a positive note, in
September, CPI inflation (1.9% m/m, 27.9% y/y) slowed more than the
market expected, and exports rebounded.
LATIN AMERICA
Inflationary pressures slightly up across the region. In Brazil, headline
CPI inflation accelerated to 4.6% y/y from 4.2% y/y in August (above the

central bank's 4.5% target), partly reflecting the reversal of a tempora-
ry electricity price discount in August. In Mexico, inflation rose to 3.5%
in September from 3.3% in August, while in Colombia it reached 6.3%,
slightly up from 6.2% in August. Inflation remained high in Chile but
was stable at 4.1%. In Peru, it was also slightly up (4.2% vs. 4.1%); the
central bank kept its policy rate unchanged at 4.25% for the thirteenth
consecutive month but maintained an upward bias amid rising inflation

and energy prices.
COMMODITIES
Energy: Limited relief. Continuation of skirmishes in the Gulf of Hormuz
and escalation of the conflict between the Houthis and a Saudi-led
coalition left oil prices fluctuating around $100/b for Brent. However,
the complex security situation has been driving the cost of shipping to
record high levels and the price of physical barrel to early April levels.
Late on Friday, President Trump announced an agreement with Russian

President Putin to release to global markets 4.8 million tonnes of die -
sel (roughly 8 days of European consumption), provided conditions in
refineries allow for it, of which 300,000 tonnes immediately. The White
House also announced a suspension of sanctions on Russia’s exports
of diesel until April 2027. Energy experts expect a de minimis price
impact.
European gas prices (TTF) rose by 11.5% over the week to 82 €/MWh.
EU gas inventories reached 73.6% of full capacities on October 11 th

(the official target is 80% at end October).
Food inflation rose sharply in September. The FAO’s Food Price Index
grew by 5.8% y/y, up from just 3% in August. This was driven by cereal
(+17% y/y) and sugar prices (+15%).

Document 226: Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13023135     1

Economy 
Entering the age of the older consumer 
01 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• The older consumer is moving center stage. America's population has been aging for decades, but the shift has accelerated: the 
share aged over 60 increased by almost 10 percentage points (pp) between 1995 and 2025. And the Census Bureau projects the 
share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population. 
• More free time means a different spending mix. People aged 65 and over spend around two and a half fewer hours per day 
working and roughly two hours more on leisure than the overall adult population. Bank of America data shows that older 
households allocate relatively more of their card spending to groceries and travel, and less to restaurants, gasoline, general 
merchandise and clothing. 
• Households aged over 55 held close to $140 trillion in net worth in the second quarter of 2026, giving many older consumers 
considerable capacity to spend on travel and other leisure categories. But wealth is not evenly distributed, and healthcare and 
other cost pressures remain significant for older households on tighter or fixed incomes.

Population aging is nothing new, but it is accelerating  
The US population has been growing relatively “older” for a long time. Exhibit 1 shows that the proportion of the population 
under 20 years old shrank from the mid-1960s through 2025. At the same time, the proportion of the population in older age 
groups has been rising.  
Exhibit 1: Over time the US population has steadily aged  
Proportion of US population by age range (%)

Source: Human Mortality Database; Bank of America Institute calculations 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

### Page 2

2 01 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Additionally, there has been a notable acceleration in the relative aging of the population in recent years. For example, between 
1995 and 2025, the proportion of the population aged over 60 rose by almost 10 percentage points (pp) to 25%.  
Several factors have driven this. First, fertility rates in the US, as in much of the world, have fallen sharply. This has slowed 
natural population growth and, by reducing the number of younger people relative to older cohorts, has increased the share of 
the population at older ages. Second, more recently, net inward migration to the US has slowed. Because migrants tend to be 
younger than the overall population, weaker immigration also tends to raise the average age. Finally, rising life expectancy over 
much of the post-war period has allowed more people to survive into older age, further increasing the older population's share.  
Exhibit 2: The trend towards an older composition of the population is expected to continue  
Forecast changes in the share of population by age group relative to 2025 (pp)

Source: United States Census Bureau 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Lower fertility and, more recently, weaker net migration look set to slow overall US population growth in the decades ahead. As a 
result, older Americans are likely to play an increasingly important role in the economy, both as a share of the population and, in 
many age groups, in absolute numbers. The Census Bureau projects the share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 
2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population (Exhibit 2). For businesses, understanding how the needs and spending patterns 
of older consumers differ from those of younger generations will therefore become increasingly relevant. 
Older age groups allocate their time differently  
Why does this matter? One reason is that older people tend to spend their time and money quite differently from younger adults. 
Most obviously, many work less or have retired altogether. Data from the American Time Use Survey (ATUS) shows that people 
aged 65 and over spend, on average, around two and a half fewer hours per day working than the population aged 15+ (Exhibit 
3). 
That leaves considerably more time for other activities. People aged 65 and over spend around two hours more per day on 
leisure and sports activities, with television viewing accounting for a particularly large share of this additional leisure time 
(Exhibit 4).

Older households also spend differently  
Bank of America internal data on credit and debit card spending provides insights into how these differences in older 
generations’ use of time also impact their spending. Rather than splitting our spending data by generation, as we often do, here 
we split by age bands, allowing for consistent comparisons over time.  
Exhibit 5 shows how the composition of card spending of households aged 61-75 and those older than 75 differs from that of all 
households. A few things stand out. For one, older households devote a greater share of their overall card spending to groceries 
(food and beverages). Households aged 61-75 also allocate more spending to travel (airlines and lodging). And restaurants and 
bars, gasoline, general merchandise and clothing account for a smaller share of older households’ card spending.  
These differences may seem fairly intuitive. Older households have more leisure hours, so spend relatively more on travel to 
enjoy them. Previously we found that more available time may support travel particularly around retirement, although the 
discretionary spending lift can be temporary (for more, read our publication: Paychecks to pensions: The evolution of retiree 
spending).  
Older households are also likely to have less need to update their work wardrobes, so they probably spend less on clothing.

Still, older households’ relatively lower spending on restaurants and bars is a little more curious, perhaps, given dining out could 
be one way of making use of more leisure time. Indeed, when we look at how these spending shares are evolving, we find a 
nuanced picture. While households aged 61-75 do spend relatively less on restaurants and bars than the overall population, they 
appear to be narrowing this gap. But for those older than 75 this is not the case. In our view, the pandemic may still be weighing 
on dining-out spending among this older age group (Exhibit 6).

Aging presents opportunities, but also challenges 
In our view, the aging of the US population presents important opportunities for the economy and businesses. Older consumers 
have more leisure time and businesses that cater to these demands may therefore benefit. 
Moreover, some older consumers hold considerable amounts of net wealth and have been some of the biggest beneficiaries of 
recent rises in equities. Using data from the Federal Reserve, Exhibit 8 shows that as of the second quarter of 2026 the net 
worth of those households aged 55 and over was close to $140 trillion, around three-quarters of the total. And with equity 
markets rising strongly, these age groups have seen a more than 20% increase in their net worth over the past two years, 
potentially enhancing their spending power further.  
But an important caveat is that not all older consumers are in this position. Previous Institute work found evidence consistent 
with equity-market wealth effects being concentrated toward higher-income households (read our publication: Paychecks to 
pensions: The evolution of retiree spending). And Census Bureau research has found that around 14% of Social Security 
recipients aged over 65 rely on it for over 90% of their income. For older households without a wealth cushion, recent elevated 
cost pressures, like gasoline, will likely have put them under more significant financial strain. They will also be more dependent 
on cost-of-living (COLA) increases in Social Security to underpin their spending.  
And, more broadly, outside of card spending, healthcare costs are a much larger factor in older households’ budgets (Exhibit 9). 
The evolution of these costs going forward will be a key variable for older consumers’ budgets.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
• The Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index* has edged up slightly since late
August, rising 0.2pts to 159.1.
• Quarterly growth momentum has
seen a further lift and is now tracking
towards a 1.7% gain for the September
quarter which would mark the strongest

quarterly rise since June 2025.
• However, both the monthly and weekly
reads show momentum has faded over
the course of the quarter. After posting
0.7-0.9%mth gains through May, June
and July, the Index rose just 0.2%
in August and is tracking a similarly
subdued 0.3% pace for September.
Weekly reads are also tracking a more
moderate trend pace of 0.2%mth.
• On a quarterly basis, the detail shows
discretionary services and international
transactions have led the pick-up.

While some of this looks to be catch-
up from travel disruptions in the June
quarter, gains have been consistent and
broadly based. Likewise, there is little
evidence that the pick-up is coming from
a lift in prices with recent CPI updates
suggesting services inflation has been
steady rather than accelerating (see
here).
• On the apparent loss of momentum
over the course of the quarter, the detail
suggests this is a re-run of the fuel price
squeeze in March-April-May with the

essentials spend gain rising strongly. So
far the spillover effects are showing up
mainly as a dampening in discretionary
goods spend with the slowing in
discretionary services largely confined to
hospitality.
• Overall, the card tracker data suggests
the Q3 spending data will still come in
as a relatively solid gain, even allowing
for a firmer gain in prices in the quarter.
However, the fade in momentum is
notable with the impact of further fuel

pricer increases and this week’s RBA rate
rise still to come. Add what now looks
more likely to be a follow-on interest
rate hike in November and the December
quarter is shaping as a significant
slowdown in card activity.

and weekly reads show
momentum has faded over
the course of the quarter.”

• Our MHSI and retail card indexes 
are composite measures based on 
transactions in categories that are in 
scope for the ABS monthly household 
spending indicator (MHSI) and retail 
sales estimates (which have been based 
on the MHSI detail since the ABS retail 
survey was discontinued in June 2025). 
• As noted previously, the retail and 
MHSI index measures significantly 
outperformed the broader WCTI in 
Q2 but with the gap narrowing in July-
August. The latest weekly signals now 
show both more in line at 1.6%qtr and 
1.7%qtr respectively. 
• The retail card index measure is a 
narrower subset of spend that excludes 
fuel and most services except hospitality. 
Chart 6 shows a sharp weakening in 
non food retail in recent weeks with 
food retail also stalling but hospitality 
tracking a firmer, albeit slowing pace. 
• The ABS MHSI was a little firmer than 
we expected but came in below market 
expectations in August with a flat result, 
the 3mth growth rate ticking up to 
2.2%qtr. Note that a significant part of the 
quarterly strength is coming from vehicle 
sales which surged through the middle of 
the year (see here for more).

WESTPAC ECONOMICS 9
About the Westpac card data indicators
The indicators presented in this report are based on the millions of credit and debit card
transactions processed every day. Transactions covering over ten million merchants are
classified into over 700 categories. These are in turn grouped into higher level aggregates

that provide a timely guide to wider economic trends.
The main focus of these indicators is consumer spending. Where possible, we have sought
to exclude ‘non spending’ transactions such as: money transfers; tax payments; loan
repayments; charitable donations; and superannuation contributions.
It should also be noted that these indicators will also be affected by shifts between card and
non-card transactions. This was a significant factor during the COVID–19 pandemic – health

concerns about the use of physical cash leading to significantly higher use of cards vs cash,
particularly where contact–less transactions are available. Transaction flows also include
reversals/refunds which were also a significant phenomenon during the onset of COVID,
especially in areas such as travel.
All transaction data is compiled at a highly aggregated level so that individual customer or
merchant data is never revealed.
Index construction

The key metrics used in this report are indexes of spending–related card activity where the
base of 100 is average activity in 2019. As an example, if transaction flows are 5% above their
average level in 2019, the index read for the period is 105. If flows in a subsequent period are
8% above the average level in 2019, the index read for this period is 108. Growth between the
two periods can be calculated simply as the change between the two index reads, i.e. 2.9%.

All measures are adjusted for regular seasonality. Weekly estimates are generated using
the US Bureau of Labor’s MoveReg weekly seasonal adjustment program. Note that in some
cases, high levels of volatility during the COVID period mean it is not possible to produce
seasonally adjusted estimates for some historical periods.
Also, note that previous versions of this report used different approaches to seasonal
adjustment and measurement more generally. This means Index reads are sometimes not

directly comparable. See the ‘About the Westpac card data indicators’ sections from earlier
reports to more detail.
Classifications
Note that the measures used for card data and in this report do not align completely with
the those used in official ABS statistics, including the ABS household spending indicator,
ABS retail trade survey and ABS estimate of consumer spending published in the national
accounts. There are a range of differences including around both coverage and classification.

As such, the card data should be treated as broadly indicative.
The transaction data is grouped into 26 categories that are then combined into four main as
follows:
Discretionary goods: alcoholic beverages, tobacco, clothing & footwear, furnishings &
household equipment, household appliances, vehicle–related, recreational & cultural goods,
newspaper, books & stationery, and other personal effects.
Discretionary services: transport services (part), recreational & cultural services, gambling,

catering services, accommodation services, other personal care, insurance & financial
services, other services.
Essential goods: food & non alcoholic beverages, medical products, appliances & equipment,
and operation of personal transport equipment.
Essential services: electricity, gas & other fuels, health services, transport services (part),
communications and education.
The report also uses two additional classifications:
MHSI/Retail: based on the extent to which categories cover activity that is in scope for the

ABS monthly household spending indicator and ABS retail trade survey.

往低。

《升息不利地產REITs，但租賃期短者表現較佳》
利率變動會直接牽動房地產投資信託基金 (REITs)的
融資成本增加 ，壓縮可分配現金流與股利 ，依高盛

研究報告顯示，從2006年起的10年期美國國債收益
率上升至少40個基點的滾動六個月週期內，REITs的
表現比標普 500指數低 6.7%，過去五年 ，大多數
REIT 與 10 年期公債殖利率呈負相關，主要因為受
到租賃期間長短的影響 ，其中以飯店業及倉儲設施
因租賃期間較短可當下反應租金變動 ，購物中心因
經濟表現佳 ，可支配所得增加 ，所以表現反而較佳 ；
淨租賃、醫療保健REITs表現最差，但以近期而言，
業績 及營運模式的調整重要性也上升 ，醫療 保健
REITs受個別基本面不同，部分業者營運模式從過去
較長天期的淨租賃轉往養老住宅 ，縮短租賃期，老
年人口財富負擔能力提升，支撐老人住房市場定價

《預期地產交易價格降溫 ，4Q26交易量增長放緩 》

Green Street CPPI 數據顯示，全美商業地產價格
相較於低點已呈現微幅回升 ，8月上漲0.8%。過去
一年累計上漲5%，但美國國債收益率的上升及對借
貸成本的影響 ，恐促使購房者重新考慮支付意願 ，
預計漲勢即將降溫。依高盛統計截至8月的季度累計
地產交易量年增 18%(扣除合併 )，高於世邦魏理仕
和仲量聯行對 3Q26投資銷售額年增 13%和14%的
預期，8月份的強勁成長主要由工業地產和辦公地產
推動，而多戶住宅市場仍然落後 。工業倉儲主要受
惠供應過剩逐漸下降 ，租賃活動受到美國製造業回
流、近岸外包與第三方物流供應鏈重組支撐 ，優質
近港與門戶型物流中心空置率開始回落；辦公室物
業則呈現科技與專業服務企業偏好承租一線 A級商
辦，而不青睞 B/C級商辦 ，拆除舊型辦公室 、改建
住宅等 。多戶住宅仍在消化陽光帶 (德州、佛州等)
過剩的存量，壓抑短期租金漲幅 。受30年期固定房
貸利率在9月升至7%的高位及去年基期也高的影響 ，
預期4Q26交易量放緩。

《REITs股息吸引力低，轉注意現金流成長動能》
美REITs的回報在利率下降時會擴張估值，但現美國
10年期公債殖利率多 在5%以上，美國REITs平均股
息收益率約3.9%，相對不具投資吸引力 ，對房地產
持中立。然若考量租金現金流增長 ，租金定價力相
對強勢的族群主要為養老住宅 ，同店淨營利年增率
可達20%上下，但估值偏高，建議回檔可留意。

主題報告概述 : 本篇報告將分析 2026年美元現況 ，最終形成
2027年美元看法。以下為本篇報告內容概要:
(1) 匯市現況：美國伊朗戰爭導致通膨疑慮再起，美元走強。
(2) 政治議題：2026年共和黨失去絕對執政對美元的影響。
(3) 期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰爭。
(4) 美元基本面：景氣與通膨循環將維持在金髮女孩經濟環境。
(5) 美元技術面：技術面跌破年線，籌碼面偏多但8月轉弱。
(6) 非美匯市: 歐洲政治面利空及日本阻貶為美元提供底部支撐
匯市結論: 美元已從2026年初的降息交易轉向受到美伊戰爭 、

油價上漲及通膨預期升溫支撐 ，但此波強勢更多來自地緣政治
與高利率環境 ，而非美國基本面出現結構性轉強 。期中選舉後 ，
共和黨失去完全執政優勢 ，國會分裂將限制大規模財政擴張 ，
有助抑制通膨與赤字惡化 ，但也降低進一步刺激經濟成長的動
能。展望2027年，美國仍面臨債務上限 、財政協商、貿易政策、
美伊衝突及烏俄戰爭等風險 ，其中地緣政治與油價走勢仍是左
右美元的重要變數 。2027年美元並非進入新一輪強勢多頭週期 ，
而是在高利率支撐下維持區間整理 。2027年美國經濟基準情境
穩健，景氣溫和成長 ，通膨從高檔放緩 。若無重大地緣政治升
級事件，美元指數預期維持97–105區間震盪，並呈現「上有壓
力、下有支撐 」的盤整格局 。當地緣政治升溫時美元偏強 ，當
通膨回落與利率下降時美元偏弱。

(1)匯市現況 : 美伊戰爭導致通膨疑慮再起，
美元走強
匯市現況: 2026年美元走勢主要受到美國經濟基本面、Fed貨幣
政策預期、川普政策以及地緣政治風險交互影響。
2026年初，川普重返貿易壁壘政策 、美國就業動能放緩且通膨
趨穩，市場普遍預期降息循環延續 ，使美元維持偏弱整理格局 ，
美元指數於1月27日一度跌至全年低點95.55。期間宣布華許出
任Fed主席的消息曾短暫帶動美元反彈 ，但整體仍未擺脫區間
震盪格局 。然而，2月28日伊朗事件爆發 ，3月9日西德州原油
一度接近每桶 120美元，能源價格上升帶動全球通膨預期升溫 ，
美國降息預期下降。進入3-4月，美國經濟數據進一步展現韌性，
製造業 、消費及投資表現改善 ，Fed季度預測亦同步上修 GDP

成長與通膨展望 。受此影響 ，利率期貨隱含年底前降息預期由
原先降息2碼逐步修正至不降息 ，推動美元於2Q26展開反彈。
同時 ，受油價飆升影響 ，9月Fed季度預測將今年 PCE上修至
3.7%，官員點陣圖亦上修利率路徑 ，市場開始討論通膨壓力可
能迫使Fed重新升息 。匯市焦點亦逐漸轉向新任聯準會主席華
許提出的新貨幣政策框架 。在Fed政策預期持續調整下 ，美元
指數於6月24日升至全年高點101.8，隨後進入高檔盤整格局。

(2)政治議題：2026年共和黨失去絕對執政對
美元的影響(2/3)
數席次機率下行趨勢可見 ，當川普政策面無法政治上快速解決
問題並且影響民生時，取得參眾議院的機率加速下行。
期中選舉結果對美元影響 ：觀察1970年以來美國期中選舉結束
後一年美元之平均表現。
民主黨主導兩院 ：當民主黨期中選舉後在參眾議院均為多數 ，
美元指數平均下跌 -4.6%，6次有4次為下跌 ，且前一年美元均
下跌。1971、1987及2007年為下跌幅度最大的3年。
1971年尼克森(共和黨)總統宣布停止黃金兌換 ，10%進口附加
關稅，迫使盟友重啟貿易與貨幣談判(最終達成史密松寧協定)，
美元下跌-7.8%。

1987年雷根(共和黨)延續1985年廣場協議後美元貶值趨勢延續 。
雙赤字壓力 、通膨升溫 、利率上升以及外資拋售壓力導致發生
1987年10月19日黑色星期一股市崩盤 ，美元指數當年下跌 -
17.6%(1986年下跌-16.1%)。
2007年布希(共和黨)總統時期面臨雙赤字 、非美利差縮小 ，以
及次貸風暴風險升溫。美元下跌-8.3%(2006年下跌-8.2%)。

Thematic Report
重點提示
(2)政治議題：2026年共和黨失去絕對執政對
美元的影響(3/3)
共和黨、民主黨各主導一議院 ：當美國國會進入兩黨各主導一
議院時期，美元指數期中選舉過後一年平均上漲 3.6%，4次有3
次為上漲，且前一年4次美元均為上漲。美國期中選舉若造成國
會由不同政黨掌控 ，通常會限制總統隨意推動大規模擴張性財
政政策與赤字支出 。這類財政節制有助於降低通膨失控風險 ，
進而支撐美元走強。4次國會分裂時段其中較大美元升值趨勢為
1983年雷根(共和黨)時期。 1980年以來聯準會主席保羅伏克爾
採取強力升息手段打擊通膨 ，美國利率保持在相對高檔一段時
間且通膨已逐步受到控制 (從1980年15%降至4%以下)。美元

1983年升值14.9%(1982年升值12.1%)。1983年後，美國經濟
順利擺脫先前的嚴重衰退 ，展開強勁增長 ，市場對美國資產信
心大增，形成所謂強勢美元格局 。美元指數在80年代高點轉折
時期美國實質利率(基準利率-消費者物價指數)已從高檔下降。
小結：2026年期中選舉結果可能形成國會分裂 ，國會分裂長期
有助抑制赤字與通膨 ，因此長期對美元有利 ，但短期可能因預
算協商及債務上限僵局增加政治風險 ，對美元造成波動。2027
年將再度面臨美國債務上限 ，以及美伊地緣政治 、貿易戰的變
數均將牽動實質利率的變化 ，因此接下來將進一步分析選後影
響美元的關鍵因子。

Thematic Report
重點提示
(3)期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰
爭(1/3)
期中選舉過後影響美元的關鍵因子聚焦 財政面 (政府關門事件)、
貿易戰(法院裁定關稅無效後宣布以其他方式課徵關稅)、地緣政
治面(伊朗戰爭觸發油價上揚)。
財政面：8月國會通過持續撥款決議至 12月11日，代表政府關
門的風險將落在11月3日後，但新國會於2027年1月3日任職，
因此很可能選後再延後撥款決議 。除此之外 ，根據兩黨政策中
心預測，2027年9月後可能再度面臨美國債務上限議題 ，屆時
面臨2028年預算審核，參眾議院的分治恐延長達成共識的時間
以及提升美國主權債信評或展望調降風險 。從全國納稅人聯盟

(NTU)情境分析預測，美國聯邦政府收支很可能在2027年2月至
7月觸及債務上限。一旦債務達到法定限額，財政部長將宣布暫
停發行債務期 。在暫停發行債務期間內 ，財政部不得增加受法
定限額約束的債務 ，但到期債務仍可展期 。計算美國財政部透
過動用收入 、現有現金儲備和採取非常規措施 ，真正無法履行
聯邦義務約在2028年2月。然而債務上限和債務違約日(X-Date)
何時達到存在很大的不確定性 。受債務上限約束的債務將於
2027年3月接近法定上限 500億美元以內，之後隨著4月稅收的
到來而暫時下降 ，但若政府財政赤字增加超過原先預測 ，X-
Date將會提前到來。

涉及特定投資標的之建議。
Thematic Report
重點提示
(3)期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰
爭(2/3)
美國信評方面 ，惠譽8月將美國信評維持在 AA+評級且展望穩
定，但警告信評下調風險因子如下 ：政府債務佔GDP水準或償
債能力惡化 ，例如未能解決公共支出和收入的平衡 ，或遭受宏
觀金融衝擊 。經濟政策制定可信度下降導致削弱美元儲備貨幣
地位，從而降低政府融資彈性 。惠譽預期 ，美國債務占GDP比
重到2028年可能升至123%，較AA評級國家46.3%的債務比率
中值高出一倍以上 ，因此，美國信評或展望下修的風險有可能
同時伴隨財政惡化或經濟成長放緩 /衰退造成償債能力惡化 。
2027年美國財政議題將繼續面臨逆風 ，共和黨失去完全執政的

環境對財政有利但政治僵局則凸顯債務上限及違約的風險 ，因
此增加美元短期波動風險。
貿易戰：2026年川普貿易戰從對等關稅全面性課徵轉向法律攻
防，預期2027年貿易政策以各國與美國雙邊貿易協定談判為主
軸，以及針對進口品項實施不同的關稅 (川普8月宣布2027年起
對一系列加拿大汽車產品加徵最高達 50%的關稅)。川普貿易政
策不確定性因素延續 ，但本行認為時序進入 2027年貿易戰全面
性惡化的可能性已降低 ，且企業及投資人已對貿易政策不確定
性有一定程度的認知 ，因此傳導至資產價格的影響性亦將有所
降低。
地緣政治 ：地緣政治風險的擴散效應高於上述財政及關稅 。主
因荷姆茲海峽能源輸出量佔全球 20%，油價上漲對通膨的影響
為2027年美元走勢的主要變數 。2月底事件爆發以來原油遠期
曲線現貨溢價 ，代表短期內市場需求強烈或發生供給中斷 。目
前美伊戰爭從熱戰轉向封鎖荷姆茲海峽 。UAE及部分中東國家
已開始運用地面輸油管線避開該管線 ，但伊朗無人機打擊範圍
遠至含蓋紅海 ，倘若事件面惡化 ，紅海運輸除了葉門胡塞武裝

組織干擾，亦有伊朗無人機攻擊風險。目前美伊談判仍未達成
✓

Thematic Report
重點提示
(3)期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰
爭(3/3)
協議，伊朗拒絕交出高濃縮鈾 ，因此仍無法排除美國運用地面
部隊進行搜索 ，也無法排除伊朗透過遠端無人機攻擊美國在其
他中東國家的軍事或煉油設施 ，美國運用地面部隊恐將地緣政
治風險升級，屆時油價、利率以及通膨預期可能同步向上。
風險變數對2027年美元的影響 ：美國政府債務問題並非全新風
險因子。自1960年以來，美國國會已經累計調整法定債務上限
超過78次。然而，2027年若期中選後國會分裂 ，疊加年底預算
勢必面臨再延後撥款決議 ，屆時政府關門或違約的不確定性因
素將影響經濟成長並且降低聯準會緊縮的可能 。而美國債務上

限再度上調則可能引起信評展望調降的風險 。以美元的角度而
言，有紀律的財政計畫的確可將加強投資人對美元資產的信心 ，
對美元有利 。但短線若因此造成政府關門以及無法在預算達成
協議則對美元短線不利 ，因此定調2027年財政風險因子在分裂
國會環境，對美元而言屬於短空長多。
除了債務風險外 ，貿易戰及美伊戰爭事件的發展最終將反應在
油價及通膨的走勢。從今年油價一度飆升至每桶120美元可見，
短期油價的上漲並未造成短線通膨危機。但不可否認，高油價

若債務上限 、地緣政治 、貿易壁壘等議題未惡化 ，預期美國經
濟偏向溫和成長 ，通膨可望放緩 。觀察中國信託美國景氣動向
指數可見，3個月以來，股市動能略顯放緩，但多數領先指標仍
持續改善 。FactSet 預估2027年標普500企業獲利成長 15%，
且營收成長高達9%，企業獲利面維持高度擴張。樂觀的企業前
景繼續帶動資本支出 ，彭博社統計2027年標普500企業資本支
出年增率仍高達 20%，正向的企業獲利持續傳導到實際經濟 。
2024年以來，實際經濟數據持續改善 ，且今年已回到 0軸以上
擴張區間 。伴隨調查及價格指標持續向上 ，整體景氣動能趨勢

正向。 展望2027年，溫和成長伴隨通膨放緩美元處於盤整偏弱
格局，基本情境內貨幣政策則傾向維持基準利率穩定。

義大利民粹政黨執政時期10
年公債殖利率與歐元區利差
30%，歐洲政治生態將進入高度不穩定與兩極化的狀態 。在歐
洲多黨內閣制或雙首長制國家中 ，30%是傳統主流政黨能否順
利執政的關鍵臨界點 。進入2027年，歐盟將有 7國舉行國會選

舉，1月30日德國總統選舉以及4月18日法國總統大選。民粹主
義右翼政黨正在崛起 ，並可能在多個國家大幅增加席次 。每次
選舉結果都將影響各國政治局勢 ，進而反映整個歐洲的政治環
境。然而，右翼民粹主義的崛起對金融市場也並非絕對利空 ，
義大利右翼政黨取得政權後堅持財政紀律 ，向金融市場展現負
責任的治理 ，同時關注移民和文化衝突等社會議題 ，以維繫基
層支持者 。義大利和歐元區 10年期公債利差從義大利右翼政黨
勝選時2.45%至今已收斂至0.83%。此外，許多右翼民粹主義政
黨也緩和了其疑歐立場 ，不再主張退出歐元區或歐盟 。2027年
法國總統大選為首要政治議題。法國極右翼領袖勒龐(Le Pen)與
她所屬的政黨國民聯盟 (RN)近年來調整了政綱 ，已放棄脫歐主
張，並且在財政上淡化激進政策 ，強調財政紀律將遵守歐盟規
範，將財政赤字控制在GDP 3%以下。整體而言，歐洲政治光譜
進入兩極化 ，法國選後議會仍需籌組多數聯盟 ，財政惡化風險
可控，但若極左派梅蘭雄意外勝選，大幅增加政府支出、對富

Thematic Report
重點提示
(6)非美匯市 : 歐洲政治不確定性及日本阻貶為
美元提供底部支撐(5/5)
✓
日圓長期轉為多頭
關鍵因子則將落在
日本政府是否開始
運用政策引導海外
投資資金回流
可視為第一波技術面日圓多方關卡。就基本面而言，政府干預匯
市無法改變貨幣政策與財政政策的路徑。若日本通膨無法帶動日
本央行加速升息，日圓利率水準則無法形成資金流入的誘因。以
日圓長期轉為多頭關鍵因子則將落在日本政府是否開始運用政策
引導海外投資資金回流(日本財務省統計到2025年底，日本在海
外的淨資產達近562兆日圓)，將是長期影響日圓走勢的最有效政
策。
匯市結論: 美元已從2026年初的降息交易轉向受到美伊戰爭、油

價上漲及通膨預期升溫支撐 ，但此波強勢更多來自地緣政治與
高利率環境 ，而非美國基本面出現結構性轉強 。期中選舉後 ，
共和黨失去完全執政優勢 ，國會分裂將限制大規模財政擴張 ，
有助抑制通膨與赤字惡化 ，但也降低進一步刺激經濟成長的動
能。展望2027年，美國仍面臨債務上限 、財政協商、貿易政策
及美伊衝突等風險 ，其中地緣政治與油價走勢仍是左右美元的
重要變數 。外部變數則關注烏俄戰爭是否停戰 、美日聯和干預
日圓與日本2028財年赤字規模 ，以及法國總統大選對歐洲政治
光譜的影響。
整體而言，2027年美元並非進入新一輪強勢多頭週期 ，而是在
高利率支撐下維持區間整理 。2027年美國經濟基準情境穩健 ，
景氣溫和成長 ，通膨從高檔放緩 。若無重大地緣政治升級事件 ，
美元指數預期維持 97-105區間震盪，並呈現「上有壓力、下有
支撐」的盤整格局 。當地緣政治升溫時美元偏強 ，當通膨回落
與利率下降時美元偏弱。
17

新興国

2026 年 10 月 5 日　全 9 頁

ドル高・米金利上昇への転換で問われる新
興国通貨の耐性 
耐性は、メキシコ、マレーシア、ブラジルで比較的高い

経済調査部 シニアエコノミスト 増川 智咲

[要約] 
 2026 年の新興国通貨は、中東危機を背景とする原油高とリスク回避姿勢の強まり、米
国の実質金利上昇に伴う対米金利差の縮小という共通のショックを受けたが、通貨間
のパフォーマンスには差があった。 
 2026年4～6月には、多くの国で交易条件や輸出入数量の動向、サービス・所得収支を
含む経常収支の構造が、通貨の強弱と概ね整合した。ただし、インド、南アフリカ
（南ア） 、トルコでは、経常収支だけでは為替動向を説明できず、資金フローやインフ
レ期待などの金融面の影響を大きく受けた。また、2026 年 7 月以降は、ドル安に加え、
各国の利上げや為替介入等が通貨を下支えしてきた。 
 9 月 16 日の米国の利上げと追加利上げ観測は、新興国通貨にとって再びリスクとなり
うる。今後の耐性を総合すると、メキシコ、マレーシア、ブラジルは、経常収支、外
貨獲得力、直接投資の規模、外貨準備の厚みなどの面で相対的に下支え材料が多い。
一方、インドネシアとフィリピンは厚い外貨準備を有するものの、経常赤字と市場性
資金の変動に注意が必要である。タイはエネルギー高に対して脆弱ではあるが、外貨
獲得力や直接投資、外貨準備の厚みが緩衝材になる見通しである。対象国の中では、
海外借入依存度の高さ、期待インフレの高さ、低い政策信認という点で、トルコの脆
弱性が最も大きい。

### Page 2

1

2 / 9

2026 年の新興国通貨：共通ショックの後に鮮明となった国別格差 
2026 年の新興国通貨を取り巻く主なリスクには、中東危機を背景とする原油高とリスク回避
姿勢の強まり、米国の実質金利上昇に伴う対米金利差の縮小、ドル高への転換などが挙げられ
る。ただし、各要因の強さは時期によって大きく変化した。このため、本稿では新興国通貨の
対ドル為替レートの動向を 3 つの局面に分けて整理する（図表 1） 。 
第 1 の局面は、中東危機が生じる前の①2025 年 12 月末～2026 年 2 月 28 日である。この時期
は、米連邦準備制度理事会（FRB）の独立性をめぐる懸念や利下げ期待などを背景にドル安が
進み、多くの新興国通貨が対ドルで上昇した。 
第2の局面は、②米国によるイラン攻撃から、4月8日の米国・イラン間の初回停戦発表まで
である。中東情勢の緊迫化に伴う原油価格の上昇と、金融市場におけるリスク回避姿勢の強ま
りを受け、新興国通貨は対ドルで幅広く下落した。 
第 3 の局面は、③初回停戦発表から FRB による利上げ直前（2026 年 9 月 11 日）までである。
ただし、この期間も中東情勢は完全には安定せず、為替市場を動かす要因は変化した。 
本稿では、第 3 局面を 4～6 月期の③-1 と、7 月から FRB 利上げ直前の 9 月 11 日までの③-2
とし、前者については貿易・経常収支、後者についてはドル相場、金利差、資本フロー、政策
対応を軸として検討する。

（注 2）米ドルは、プラスが実効レートの上昇を表す。

通貨間格差の要因①～貿易・経常収支を通じた影響 
まず、③-1 にあたる 2026 年 4～6 月期について、通貨パフォーマンスの差を貿易・経常収支
の面から確認する。図表2～4は、各国の経常収支と貿易収支の変動要因を示している。資源価
格の上昇などに伴う交易条件の変化だけでなく、輸出入数量、サービス収支、所得収支も併せ
て確認する。

経常収支悪化と通貨安が整合的だったタイ、インドネシア、フィリピン 
③-1 の期間に対ドルで為替レートが下落した国のうち、タイ、インドネシア、フィリピンで
は、2026 年に入って経常赤字（GDP 比）が拡大した（図表 2） 。経常赤字の拡大は、これらの通
貨が軟調に推移したことと整合的である。 
タイとフィリピンでは、貿易赤字の拡大が経常収支悪化の主因となった。背景には、エネル
ギー価格上昇による交易条件の悪化と、輸入数量の増加がある（図表 3、4） 。両国は、本稿の
対象国の中でもエネルギー輸入への依存度が高い。加えて、AI ブームを背景に電子機器などの
輸出向け生産が拡大し、中間財輸入が増加した可能性がある。ただし、輸出向け中間財の輸入
増は、将来の生産・輸出拡大に伴う側面もあるため、外部脆弱性の悪化と一概に評価できない
点には留意が必要である。 
インドネシアでは、原油高の局面においても貿易黒字を維持したが、黒字幅が縮小し、経常
赤字の拡大につながった。貿易黒字縮小の原因には、交易条件の悪化に加え、輸出数量の減少
があった。同国の輸出構造は資源の割合が比較的高く、マレーシアに比べて AI関連の電子機器
輸出拡大の恩恵を受けにくい。最大の輸出先である中国の内需減速も、輸出の下押し要因とな
ったとみられる。

経常収支だけでは通貨安を説明しにくいインドとトルコ 
経常収支の動向だけで通貨安を十分に説明できない国がインドとトルコである。インドはエ
ネルギーの純輸入国であり、2026 年 4～6 月期の財の貿易赤字の規模は GDP 比 10％近くにまで
拡大した。一方、IT・ビジネスサービスを中心とするサービス収支と、海外送金を中心とする
第二次所得収支の黒字が財の貿易赤字の相当分を相殺し、経常赤字は比較的小幅にとどまった。 
トルコもエネルギーの純輸入国であることから、2026年4～6月期は交易条件が悪化した。た
だし、高金利による需要抑制が消費財輸入を抑え、貿易赤字と経常赤字はいずれも前期比で縮
小した。それにもかかわらず、対象国の中でリラの下落率が最も大きかったことは、経常取引
以外の要因が強く作用していたことを示唆している。

### Page 4

1

4 / 9

経常取引が通貨の下支えとなったマレーシアとメキシコ 
マレーシアとメキシコでは、良好な経常取引が通貨の下支えとなったとみられる。両国は
2026年4～6月期に貿易黒字・経常黒字を維持した。マレーシアでは天然ガス価格の上昇に伴う
交易条件の改善に加え、半導体・電子機器などの輸出数量の増加が寄与した。同国の貿易黒字
は GDP 比 7.5％に達し、経常黒字を支えた。 
メキシコでは、米国向けを中心とする工業品輸出の増加が貿易黒字に大きく寄与した。ニア
ショアリングを背景とする生産拠点の集積も、輸出基盤を支えているとみられる。両国では、
堅調な財の輸出を背景とした外貨獲得力が、通貨の下支え材料となった。

経常赤字の「質」が異なるブラジルと、経常取引だけで通貨高を説明できない南ア 
ブラジルでは、資源価格の上昇に伴う交易条件の改善により、2026年4～6月期の貿易黒字幅
は前期から拡大した。一方、外国企業への配当支払いや国債の利子払い等による第一次所得収
支の大幅な赤字を背景に、経常収支は赤字となった。ブラジルの経常赤字は、輸出競争力の低
下やエネルギー輸入の増加に起因するものではない。この点で、貿易赤字の拡大を伴う国と比
べて、対外収支面の脆弱性は相対的に小さい。 
南アフリカ（南ア）では、原油高による交易条件の悪化を受け、2026年4～6月期の貿易黒字
幅が前期から大きく縮小した。さらに、第一次所得収支の赤字規模が大きいことから、経常収
支は GDP比▲0.9％の赤字となった。経常収支が悪化した一方、ランドが対ドルで上昇した背景
には、資本フローなどの経常取引以外の要因が考えられる。 
このように、③-1 では、多くの国で、貿易・経常収支の動向が通貨パフォーマンスの差と一
定程度整合した。米国の金利が相対的に安定していて金利差による変動が目立ちにくかったこ
とに加え、エネルギー高や AIブームといった貿易・経常収支要因が主な為替変動要因だったた
めだと考えられる。 
例外は、インド、南ア、トルコである。 3 カ国では、経常収支と通貨パフォーマンスが必ず
しも一致しなかった。また 7月以降である③-2の期間になると、③-1に経常収支が悪化してい
たタイとインドネシアで為替レートが対ドルで上昇に転じた。つまり、③全体の通貨動向を説
明するには、経常取引だけでなく、金利差を意識した資本フローや政策対応など、金融面の要
因による影響が大きい可能性があるため、次節で考察する。

人投資家の証券売買や金利動向など、より高頻度の指標を用いる。 
トルコでは、2026 年 1～3 月期に資本流出圧力が強まった後、4～6 月期には証券投資やその
他投資が大幅流入超となり、中央銀行も外貨準備を積み増すなど、国際収支面では改善がみら
れた（図表 5） 。8 月の外国人売買統計でも、株式市場へは 6.5 億ドルの流入超となったことが
わかる。このように、資本フローを引き付けているにもかかわらず、リラ安が進んだ背景には、
市場参加者のインフレ期待や、将来的な通貨下落予想、政策運営への信認欠如といった、フロ
ー統計に表れにくい要因があったとみられる。また、4～6 月期に流入した資本の大半は、証券
投資やその他投資で、直接投資は流出超であった。安定的な長期資金よりも市場環境に左右さ
れやすい資金への依存は、リラ相場の不安定性を高める要因となりうる。 
インドでは2025年10～12月期以降、3四半期連続で証券投資とデリバティブを中心に流出超
となった。特に、対内証券投資（ネット）のマイナスが大きかった。それまで投資を積み増し
ていた海外投資家によるポジション調整や、対米金利差の縮小などが資金流出につながった可
能性がある。ルピー安には、経常取引よりも金融要因が強く作用した可能性が高い。 
南アは、4～6 月期に証券投資が大きく流出超となった。外国人売買統計を見ると、株式市場
からの資本流出が顕著だった。他方、比較的高い金利水準を背景に、債券市場には 4 月以降連
続して資金が流入した。外国人売買統計では、8 月に債券と株式の合計（ネット）が 85 億ラン
ドと 4 カ月ぶりのプラスとなった。ランドの上昇には、経常取引要因以上に、金利差を意識し
た債券市場への資金流入が寄与したとみられる。

2026 年 7 月からFRB 利上げ直前までの期間（③-2）に、通貨が上昇に転じた国 
③-2では、③-1に軟調だったタイバーツとインドネシアルピアが対ドルで上昇に転じ、マレ
ーシアリンギットも上昇した。短期間で経常収支の構造が大きく変化したとは考えにくいこと
から、3 通貨の持ち直しには、金融面の要因が強く作用したとみられる。 
マレーシアでは、4～6 月期の経常黒字と輸出基盤が対外収支の下支えとなる中、7 月以降の
ドル安がリンギット上昇に寄与した。非居住者による国内債券保有は 7 月に減少したものの、
8 月には増加に転じており、期間後半には債券フローもリンギットの下支え要因となった。 
タイバーツの反転には、広範なドル安が最も直接的に作用したとみられる。これに加え、 7
月には金・燃料輸入の減少を背景に経常赤字が前月から縮小したほか、電子関連輸出や観光に
も持ち直しがみられ、対外収支と景気への警戒が和らいだ可能性がある。 
インドネシアでは、2026年5・6月に累計100bpの利上げが実施されたほか、NDF・スポット・
DNDF1を通じた為替安定化策がルピアを下支えした。7 月以降は外国人投資家による国内金融資

1 NDF は、取引対象通貨の現物受け渡しを行わず、事前に定めた契約レートと決済時点の実勢レートとの差額
を、通常は米ドルで差金決済する取引。DNDF は、国内市場において、取引対象通貨の現物受渡しを行わず、事
前に定めた契約レートと決済時点の実勢レートとの差額を現地通貨で差金決済する取引。

産への資金フローにも改善がみられ、ルピアの持ち直しを下支えしたとみられる。 
図表 5 新興国への民間投資フロー

（注 1）各項目はネット。プラスが純流出、マイナスが純流入。 
（注 2）国際収支は非季節調整値。GDP は 3 四半期平均値を用いた。 
（注 3）タイの2026 年 Q2 のデータは未公表。 
（出所）各国統計より大和総研作成 
各国の対外資金調達構造：トルコの脆弱性が対象国の中で相対的に大きい 
以下では、対外債務の規模と通貨構成、対外負債の構成、ネットの対外資産・負債、外貨準
備と国内金融市場の厚み、の 4 つの観点から各国の耐性を評価する（図表 6、7） 。 
対象国の中で、ドル高局面への脆弱性が最も大きいと考えられるのはトルコである。対外債
務の規模が比較的大きく、その多くが外貨建てである。対外負債に占める銀行借入などの「そ
の他投資」の割合も58％（2025年）と高く、リスク回避局面では借換え条件の悪化や資本流出
圧力が強まりやすい（図表 7） 。 
トルコ以外の対象国では、脆弱性は相対的に限定される。マレーシアは対外債務残高の規模
が大きいものの、対外資産も厚く、対外純資産残高は GDP比▲5.5％、つまり小幅な対外純負債
にとどまる。また、対外負債の約 50％を直接投資が占め、負債構造は比較的安定している。対
外負債に占める直接投資の割合という観点では、ブラジルとタイでも高く、短期の市場性資金
への依存を抑える要因となっている。 
南アは、対外資産が対外負債を上回る純債権国であり、対外債務に占める外貨建ての割合も
低い。国内投資家層が比較的厚く、自国通貨建てで資金を調達しやすいことが、為替ミスマッ
チを抑制している。 
インドネシア、フィリピンでは、対外債務に占める外貨建ての割合が高い。ただし、債務の
長期化や為替ヘッジなどにより、ドル高による短期的な返済負担の増加は一定程度抑制されて

いるとみられる。もっとも、インドネシアやフィリピンでは、対外負債に占める証券投資の割
合が比較的大きく、リスク回避局面では短期的な資本流出が生じやすい。フィリピンは国内投
資家層の厚みが相対的に乏しく、米長期金利の上昇が国内金融環境に波及しやすい。両国は相
応の外貨準備を有し、地域金融セーフティーネットも存在するため、対外流動性リスクを緩和
する余地はある。ただし、これらは通貨下落を完全に防ぐものではなく、外部環境の悪化時に
は一時的に下落圧力が強まる可能性がある。

FRB 利上げ後の新興国通貨：相対的に有利なマレーシア、メキシコ、ブラジ
ル 
9 月 16 日の米国の利上げと追加利上げ観測は、新興国通貨にとって再びリスクとなりうるが、
その影響は一様ではないだろう。総合的に判断すると、メキシコ、マレーシア、ブラジルは相
対的に下支え材料が多い。ただし、その根拠は異なる。メキシコは工業品輸出と直接投資、マ
レーシアは経常黒字と対外資産、ブラジルは貿易黒字、直接投資、金利差（図表 8）が主な緩
衝材となる。一方、南アとインドは対外バランスシートやサービス輸出に強みを持つものの、
証券フローの変動には注意が必要である。 
インドネシアとフィリピンは、外貨準備の厚みという点でバッファーを有するが、経常赤字
や外国人投資家のリスク回避に対する通貨の感応度が高い。タイはエネルギー高に対して脆弱
ではあるが、半導体・電子機器輸出による外貨獲得能力や直接投資、外貨準備の厚みが緩衝材
になる見通しである。対象国の中で最も脆弱なのはトルコである。海外借入への依存度が高い
うえ、不安定なインフレ期待と政策への低い信認がリラの下落圧力を増幅しやすいためである。 
以上のとおり、外部環境の変化に対する新興国通貨への影響は、経常収支の黒字・赤字だけ
ではなく、外貨獲得力、資金調達方法、資本流入の安定性、外貨準備、対外債務の通貨・満期
構成、政策への信認に左右されそうである。
